23-00-00-040-801MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Preparation - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches InoperativePDF p. 25
Open original AMM PDF - page 25 ↗ 
2. MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Preparation - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Control
Wheel PTT switches inoperative.
(2) The Control Wheel PTT switches can be inoperative under these conditions:
(a) The associated Audio Control Panel PTT switch operates normally.
(b) The inoperative switch is deactivated open.
B. References
Reference Title
23-51-04-000-801 Control Wheel PTT Switch Removal (P/B 401)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Confirmation
SUBTASK 23-00-00-700-003
(1) Do the steps that follow to confirm that the inoperative control wheel PTT switch is inoperative
open:
(a) Listen for a sound during a test transmission without pushing the associated PTT switch.
NOTE: If there is sound, the PTT switch is inoperative in the closed position.
(2) Use the audio selector panel PTT switch to verify R/T and/or I/C communications.
23-00-00
E. PTT Switches Deactivation
SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-001
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2
C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT
C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2
C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT
D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O
D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT
SUBTASK 23-00-00-010-001
(2) Remove the PTT switch on the control wheel to get access to the wire
(TASK 23-51-04-000-801).
SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-001
(3) Disconnect, cap and stow the GROUND (BLK/BRN) wire from the PTT switch.
SUBTASK 23-00-00-420-001
(4) Install the PTT switch.
SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-002
(5) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2
C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT
C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2
C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT
D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O
D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT
SUBTASK 23-00-00-930-001
(6) Put an INOP placard on the PTT switch.

3. MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Restoration - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the PTT switches
inoperative.
B. References
Reference Title
23-51-04-000-801 Control Wheel PTT Switch Removal (P/B 401)
23-51-04-400-801 Control Wheel PTT Switch Installation (P/B 401)
23-00-00
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-003
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2
C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT
C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2
C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT
D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O
D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT
SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-002
(2) Remove the INOP placard from the switch.
SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-003
(3) Remove the PTT switch on the control wheel (TASK 23-51-04-000-801).
SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-004
(4) Remove the cap from the GROUND (BLK/BRN) wire for the PTT switch.
SUBTASK 23-00-00-420-002
(5) Install the new PTT switch on the control wheel. To install it, do this task: Control Wheel PTT
Switch Installation, TASK 23-51-04-400-801.
SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-004
(6) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name

C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT
C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2

23-00-00-040-807MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Preparation - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) SwitchesPDF p. 27
Open original AMM PDF - page 27 ↗ 
4. MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Preparation - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches
Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the PTT
switches on the Flight Crew Audio Control Panel inoperative.
(2) The Audio Control Panel PTT switches can be inoperative under these conditions:
(a) The associated control wheel PTT switch operates normally
(b) The inoperative switch is deactivated open.
23-00-00
B. References
Reference Title
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Confirmation
SUBTASK 23-00-00-700-001
(1) Do the steps that follow to confirm that the inoperative Audio Control Panel PTT switch is
inoperative open and the associated control wheel PTT switch operates normally:
(a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811
(b) Push the PA microphone selector switch on the associated audio control panel.
(c) Make sure that the PA MIC light comes on.
(d) Speak into the associated microphone without pushing any PTT switch.
(e) Make sure you hear no voice on the PA system.
(f) Push the inoperative PTT switch and speak into the associated microphone.
(g) Make sure you hear no voice at the other pilot's headset.
(h) Push the associated pilot's control wheel PTT switch and speak into the associated
microphone.
(i) Make sure you hear your voice clearly at the other pilot's headset.

5. MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Restoration - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches
Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the Audio Control
Panel PTT switches inoperative.
(2) To put the airplane to usual condition, replace the Audio Control Panel with the inoperative
switch.

(1) Do the tasks: AUDIO CONTROL PANEL - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION,

23-12-00-710-801VHF Communication System - Operational TestPDF p. 37

2. VHF Communication System - Operational Test
A. General
(1) The operational test is a BITE test of the VHF communication transceiver.
(a) The BITE test does a check of the internal functions of the VHF communication
transceiver. The BITE test also does a check of the ARINC 429 frequency tuning input
data to the VHF communication transceiver.
RYR 006, 007, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169,
174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341,
343, 346-506; RYR 013, 014 PRE SB 737-23-1381; RYR 507-999 PRE SB 737-23-1673
(b) The same sequence of steps is used for the No. 1 or No. 2 VHF communication system.
RYR 013, 014 POST SB 737-23-1381; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484; RYR 507-999 POST SB 737-23-1673
(c) The same sequence of steps is used for the No. 1, No. 2, or No. 3 VHF communication
system.
RYR ALL
(d) The operational test is a quick test that makes sure that the VHF transceiver is functional.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area

(a) Push and hold the SQL/LAMP TEST switch.
1) Make sure that the red CONTROL INPUT FAIL light comes on.
2) Make sure that the green LRU PASS light comes on.
(b) Release the SQL/LAMP TEST switch.
(c) Push the TEST switch.
1) Make sure that the LRU PASS light comes on.
2) Make sure that the VSWR/power display shows less than 3.0.
(d) Turn and hold the RFL/OFF/FWD switch in the FWD position.
1) Make sure that the VSWR/power display shows 25 or more for forward power.
(e) Turn and hold the RFL/OFF/FWD switch in the RFL position.

SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-015
(5) Before you do the BITE test, make sure that the VHF-3 is in the voice mode (a voice frequency
shows in the active (left) display of the RTP).
SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-002
(6) Push and release the TEST switch on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front
panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3).
(a) Make sure that these conditions occur:
1) All three LEDs on the VHF transceiver front panel turn red for about two seconds.
2) The LRU STATUS LED turns green and the CONTROL FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL
LEDs remain red for about two seconds.
3) All three LEDs go off for about two seconds.
4) The LRU STATUS LED comes on green for about 30 seconds and the CONTROL
FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL LEDs remain off.
NOTE: Any of the three LEDs that come on red after the self-test is completed
indicates a defective condition.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 965-1696-021
SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-014
(7) Push and release the TEST switch on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front
panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3).
(a) The VDR STATUS LED will turn on green and the CONTROL FAULT and ANTENNA
FAULT LEDs remain off.
NOTE: Any of the three LEDs that come on red or amber after the self-test is completed
indicates a defective condition.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 822-1250-XXX
SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-016
(8) Before you do the BITE test, make sure that the VHF-3 is in the voice mode (a voice frequency
shows in the active (left) display of the RTP).
SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-007
(9) Push and release the TEST switch on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front
panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3).
(a) Make sure that these conditions occur:
1) All three LEDs on the VHF transceiver front panel turn red for about two seconds.
2) The LRU STATUS LED turns green and the CONTROL FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL
LEDs remain red for about two seconds.
3) All three LEDs go off for about five seconds.
23-12-00

FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL LEDs remain off.

indicates a defective condition.

23-12-00-730-801VHF Communication System - System TestPDF p. 40
Open original AMM PDF - page 40 ↗ 
3. VHF Communication System - System Test
A. General
(1) This task includes these tests:
(a) A Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) Test
(b) An Radio Tuning Panel (RTP) to RTP Interface Test
(c) A Very High Frequency (VHF) Communication Test.
(2) Make sure that the flight interphone system operates. The communication test uses these flight
interphone system components: Audio Control Panel (ACP)s, headsets, microphones, and
Push-To-T alk (PTT) switches.
(3) Make sure that the airplane is not in or near any large metal structures. The position of the
airplane on the ground can decrease the quality of VHF communication. Ground equipment
and structures can also cause blocked Radio Frequency (RF) signal attenuation. If the quality
of transmitted and received voice signals is not satisfactory, move the airplane to a better
position.
(4) Make sure that you use approved test frequencies when you speak with the radio tower
operator.
B. References
Reference Title
23-27-00-700-812-009 ACARS - System Test (P/B 501)
23-27-00-700-815-001 ACARS - System Test (P/B 501)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
23-12-00
D. Prepare for the Test
SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-009

CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3

F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3
Row Col Number Name
C 10 C00284 PANEL & INSTR ELEX PANEL
SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-023
(2) If it is necessary, do these steps to change from Data to Voice:
(a) Push the applicable VHF key on RTP-1 (or, on RTP-2) on the P8 panel.
(b) Tune the applicable RTP to display “DATA” in the active window.
(c) Push the display transfer button on the RTP .
(d) Make sure that “DATA” is in the standby window of the RTP .
SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-024
(3) If it is necessary, do these steps to change from Voice to Data:
(a) Push the display transfer button on the RTP .
(b) Make sure that “DATA” toggles from the standby display to the active display on the RTP .
SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-025
(4) If it is necessary, do these steps to change back from Data to Voice:
(a) Push the display transfer button on the RTP .
(b) Make sure that “DATA” toggles from the active display to the standby display on the RTP .
E. BITE Test
SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-003
(1) Do this task: VHF Communication System - Operational Test, TASK 23-12-00-710-801.
F . RTP to RTP Interface Test
SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-003
(1) Do these steps at the RTPs:
(a) Push the VHF3 switch on each RTP-1.
(b) Push the VHF3 switch on each RTP-2.
1) Make sure that RTP-1, and RTP-2 show the same ACTIVE and STANDBY
frequencies.
(c) Set RTP-2 to a new frequency.
1) Make sure that RTP-1 shows the new frequency.
23-12-00
(d) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1 and RTP-2.
NOTE: The cross tuning light is above the display transfer switch, between the frequency
displays.
(e) Push the VHF2 switch on RTP-2.
1) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-2.
2) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1.
(f) Push the VHF1 switch on RTP-1.
1) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-1.
(g) Push the HF1 switch on RTP-2.
1) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1 and RTP-2.
NOTE: The cross tuning light is above the display transfer switch, between the
frequency displays.
(h) Push the HF2 switch on RTP-2.
(i) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-1 and RTP-2.
(j) Push the HF2 switch on RTP-1.
(k) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1 and RTP-2.
NOTE: The cross tuning light is above the display transfer switch, between the frequency
displays.
(l) Push the HF1 switch on RTP-1.
(m) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-1 and RTP-2.
G. VHF Communication Test
SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-004
(1) Do a test of the VHF-1 communication system:
(a) Connect a headset/boom microphone to the captain's jack panel.
(b) Do these steps to prepare the captain's ACP for VHF-1 system operation:
1) Push and release the VHF1 switch.
a) Make sure that the switch light comes on.
2) Push and release the VHF-1 volume control.
a) Make sure that the volume control indicator light comes on.
3) Set the volume control to the middle position.
4) Set the MASK/BOOM switch (if installed) to the BOOM position.
(c) Set the RTP-1 to an approved test frequency for VHF-1.
(d) Do these steps to do a voice communication test with a radio tower operator:
1) Push and hold a PTT switch while you speak.
a) Make sure that you hear the sidetone in the headset while you speak.
2) Release the PTT switch while you listen.
a) Make sure that the quality of the transmitted and received voice is satisfactory.
b) Do this step to make sure that the volume of the received voice will change:
turn the VHF1 volume control on the captain's ACP with no change in voice
quality.
23-12-00
<1> Turn the VHF1 volume control on the captain's ACP .
RYR 097-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220,
223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999
(e) Do these steps to do a 35 second keyline timer test:
1) Make sure that you have clearance from the tower operator to transmit for 40
seconds.
2) Push and hold the PTT switch for a minimum of 40 seconds.
a) Make sure that after about 35 seconds, the sidetone is not present in the
headset.
3) Release the PTT switch.
RYR ALL
SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-005
(2) Do a test of the VHF-2 communication system:
(a) Connect a headset/boom microphone to the first officer's jack panel.
(b) Do these steps to prepare the first officer's ACP for VHF-2 system operation:
1) Push and release the VHF2 switch.
a) Make sure that the switch light comes on.
2) Push and release the VHF2 volume control.
a) Make sure that the volume control indicator light comes on.
3) Set the volume control to the middle position.
4) Set the MASK/BOOM switch (if installed) to the BOOM position.
(c) Set the RTP-2 to an approved test frequency for VHF-2.
(d) Do these steps to do a voice communication test with a radio tower operator:
1) Push and hold a PTT switch while you speak.
a) Make sure that you hear the sidetone in the headset while you speak.
2) Release the PTT switch while you listen.
a) Make sure that the quality of the transmitted and received voice is satisfactory.
b) Do this step to make sure that the volume of the received voice will change:
<1> Turn the VHF2 volume control on the first officer's ACP .

SB 737-23-1483
SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-007
(4) To do a test of the VHF-3 communication system, do this task: ACARS - System Test,
TASK 23-27-00-700-815-001 or ACARS - System Test, TASK 23-27-00-700-812-009
NOTE: If the VHF Link Test passes, the VHF-3 communication system is operational.
RYR ALL
H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-015
(1) At each ACP , push and release a different transmitter selector switch that is not the applicable
VHF switch.
23-12-00
(a) Make sure that the related VHF switch light goes off.
NOTE: When you push a different transmitter selector switch, on an ACP , the first
transmitter switch mode is cancelled.
SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-017
(2) At each ACP , push and release the applicable VHF volume controls.
(a) Make sure that all the volume control indicator lights go off.

C. Removal Procedure
SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-008
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2
SUBTASK 23-12-11-020-001
(2) Do these steps to remove the VHF communication antenna [1]:
(a) Remove the ten bolts [2] that attach the VHF communication antenna [1] to the airplane.
CAUTION
REMOVE THE AERODYNAMIC FILLET SEAL CAREFULLY WITH THE
SEALANT REMOVAL TOOL. DAMAGE TO THE AIRPLANE SKIN OR THE
COAXIAL CABLE CAN OCCUR.
(b) While you hold the VHF communication antenna [1], use a sealant removal tool,
COM-2481 to break the fillet seal around the base of the antenna.
CAUTION
DO NOT PULL ON THE COAXIAL CABLE. CAREFULLY MOVE THE
ANTENNA. DISCONNECT THE COAXIAL CONNECTOR. THIS WILL
PREVENT DAMAGE TO THE COAXIAL CABLE.
(c) Move the VHF communication antenna [1] away from the airplane to get access to the
coaxial connector [4] on the coaxial cable.
(d) Disconnect the coaxial connector [4] from the VHF communication antenna [1] and
remove the antenna.
NOTE: For the top VHF communication antenna [1], make sure the coaxial connector [4]
does not go back through the hole in the fuselage. Make sure you attach the
coaxial connector [4] to something to prevent this.

3. VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket - Removal
Figure 402
A. General
(1) This procedure is used for the removal of antennas that use HI-TEK or Aerobond gaskets per
737-6789 SL-20-045.
(2) As full certification tests of the gaskets was not done, it is recommended to do the steps that
follow:
(a) Do a visual inspection of the gasket and the mating surface each D check.
(b) Do a check for deterioration and corrosion on the antenna and gasket after their removal.
(c) Do a check of the airplane skin for corrosion and fretting caused by the antenna gasket.
1) Remove any corrosion and fretting on the airplane skin.

23-12-11

F . Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-006
(1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2
23-12-11
SUBTASK 23-12-11-100-002
(2) Clean the airplane mating surface.
(a) Remove sealant with a sealant removal tool, COM-2481.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS
ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE FLAMMABLE.
OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) FOR SOLVENTS.
OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT PROCEDURES TO
USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(b) Clean the airplane mating surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent,
B00148, solvent, B00666, solvent, B00184, FCC-55 solvent, B01026, or solvent, B01054.
(c) Use a clean cotton wiper, G00034, to clean the airplane mating surface.
(d) Make sure that the airplane mating surface is dry.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-100-003
(3) If the mating surface has corrosion or other damage, or if the airplane mating surface has no
chemical conversion coating, do these steps to prepare the airplane mating surface:
NOTE: An alternative installation procedure is to apply corrosion inhibiting compound,
G00009, on an airplane mating surface that has no chemical conversion coating.
(a) If there is corrosion, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Aluminum and
Aluminum Alloys, TASK 51-21-31-350-801.
(b) Apply a layer of Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005, to the airplane mating
surface (TASK 51-21-41-370-802).
NOTE: Alternative chemical conversion coatings may also be used
(TASK 51-21-41-370-802).
SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-001
(4) Install a new o-ring [3].
(a) If it is necessary, lubricate the o-ring [3] with Parker Super-O-Lube lubricant, D00652.
(b) Align the o-ring [3] in the groove.
G. VHF Communication Antenna Installation
SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-002
(1) Keep one of the 10 bolts [2] out to do an electrical bond check.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-006
(2) Install the VHF communication antenna [1].
(a) Connect the coaxial connector [4] to the VHF communication antenna [1].
(b) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, to the shank and threads of 9 of the 10 bolts [2].
(c) Put the VHF communication antenna [1] in its position and install 9 of the bolts [2].
1) Tighten the bolts [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9.0 ±0.2 N·m).
SUBTASK 23-12-11-700-001
(3) Measure the resistance between the VHF communication antenna [1] base and the airplane
skin (SWPM 20-20-00).
NOTE: Use the empty bolt hole to get access to the VHF communication antenna base.
23-12-11
(a) Use a intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
(b) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-003
(4) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, to the shank and threads of the last bolt [2].
(a) Install the last bolt [2].
1) Tighten the bolt [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9.0 ±0.2 N·m).
SUBTASK 23-12-11-390-001
(5) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, around the base of the VHF communication antenna [1] to
make a corrosion fillet seal (TASK 51-31-00-390-804).
NOTE: Operators can defer the application of the aero-sealant in the antenna installation until
next “A” check or overnight maintenance stop to prevent a flight delay. The deferral of
the application of sealant must not exceed a maximum of eight weeks (SL 20-043).
(a) Let the sealant dry for the correct cure time.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-390-002
(6) Fill the extra holes on the antenna base with sealant, A00247.
NOTE: Additional holes on the antenna base were once intended to be used for jack screws
to help with the removal of the antenna. This is no longer recommended. Jack screws
could damage the airplane skin.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-007
(7) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2
H. Installation Test
SUBTASK 23-12-11-730-001
(1) Do this task: VHF Communication System - System Test, TASK 23-12-00-730-801.

USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Clean the airplane mating surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent,
B00148, solvent, B00666, solvent, B00184, FCC-55 solvent, B01026, or solvent, B01054.
(b) Use a clean cotton wiper, G00034, and clean the airplane mating surface again.
(c) Do these two steps above until the airplane mating surface is clean and dry.
23-12-11
SUBTASK 23-12-11-100-005
(3) If the mating surface has corrosion or other damage, or if the airplane mating surface has no
chemical conversion coating, then do these steps to prepare the airplane mating surface:
NOTE: An alternative installation procedure is to apply corrosion inhibiting compound,
G00009, on an airplane mating surface that has no chemical conversion coating.
(a) If there is corrosion, then, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Aluminum
and Aluminum Alloys, TASK 51-21-31-350-801.
(b) Apply a layer of Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005, to the airplane mating
surface (TASK 51-21-41-370-802).
NOTE: Alternative chemical conversion coatings specified in TASK 51-21-41-370-802
may also be used.
H. VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket Installation
SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-004
(1) Do these steps to install the VHF communication antenna [1]:
(a) Remove and discard the O-ring (if installed) from the VHF communication antenna [1].
(b) Remove the protective film from each side of the new gasket [5].
(c) Put the gasket [5] on the VHF communication antenna [1].
(d) Connect the coaxial connector [4] to the VHF communication antenna [1].
(e) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, to the shank and threads of the 10 bolts [2].
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY SEALANT TO THE EDGES OF THE ANTENNA. THE
GASKET RELEASES THE MOISTURE. IF YOU SEAL THE EDGES, THE
MOISTURE WILL STAY . THIS CAN CAUSE CORROSION DAMAGE TO
THE AIRPLANE, AND ANTENNA MATING SURFACES.
(f) Put the VHF communication antenna [1] in its position and install 9 of the 10 bolts [2].
1) If the bolts [2] are installed in domed nut plates, remove the elastomer from the
mounting holes in the gasket.
2) Tighten the bolts [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9 ±0 N·m).
NOTE: The gel material of the gasket is compressible, the bolts need to be
re-torqued 15 minutes of the installation.
(g) Fill extra holes on the antenna base with sealant, A00247.
NOTE: Extra holes on the antenna base were once intended to be used for jack screws
to help remove the antenna. This is no longer recommended. Jack screws could
damage the airplane skin.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-700-002
(2) Measure the resistance between the VHF communication antenna [1] base and the airplane
skin with a intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
(a) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm or less.
SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-005
(3) Install the last bolt [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9 ±0 N·m).
(a) Tighten the bolt [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9 ±0 N·m).
NOTE: The gel material of the gasket is compressible, the bolts need to be re-torqued 15
minutes of the installation.
23-12-11
SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-014
(4) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1

C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2

23-12-41-800-802Radio Tuning Panel - INOP Display TogglePDF p. 85
Open original AMM PDF - page 85 ↗ 
4. Radio Tuning Panel - INOP Display Toggle
A. General
(1) You can use this procedure to set or remove INOP from RTP ACTIVE/STANDBY frequency
displays. If the RTP frequency displays are normal, this procedure will change the displays to
show INOP . If the RTP frequency displays show INOP , this procedure will change the
frequency displays to normal.
NOTE: For airplanes with the Gables G7404-04 RTP installed, there is a known software
problem that causes INOP to show, even though the INOP pin option is not enabled.
This procedure can be used to remove the INOP indication for those airplanes. Boeing
recommends that the G7404-04 be replaced by the G7404-24 or G7404-124 panel at
the airlines convenience.
(2) If a communication system is not installed, you can use this procedure to set the RTP to INOP
for the applicable communication system.
23-12-41
(a) For Gables P/N G7404-0X, the RTP can be set to show INOP for VHF1, VHF2, VHF3,
HF1 or HF2.
(b) For Gables P/N G7404-2X or G7404-12X, the RTP can be set to show INOP only for
VHF3, HF1 or HF2.
(c) When the display for a communication system is changed in one RTP , the display of all
RTP's will be changed for that communication system.
(d) If this procedure is used to set frequency displays from INOP to normal, a communication
system failure will change (toggle) the applicable communication system to FAIL or back
to INOP .
(e) When RTP frequency displays show INOP , the set communication system is not on or not
installed.
(f) When RTP frequency displays show FAIL, the set communication system has failed.
(g) When RTP frequency displays show PANEL FAIL, there is a problem in the RTP , not in a
communication system. The RTP self test failed.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Radio Tuning Panel - Display Toggle Procedure
SUBTASK 23-12-41-860-023
(1) Do these steps:
(a) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2
(b) Push the RTP-1 Radio Tuning switch (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2) for the
communication system displays to be changed.
(c) Push the same Radio Tuning switch on RTP-2 (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2).
(d) Push the same Radio Tuning switch on RTP-3 (if installed) (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or
HF2).
(e) Open this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
(f) Wait a minimum of 5 seconds.
(g) Push and hold down the RTP-1 Frequency Transfer switch.
(h) Push and hold down the RTP-1 Radio Tuning switch (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2)
for the communication system displays to be changed.
23-12-41
(i) While still holding the Frequency Transfer switch and Radio Tuning system switch on
RTP-1,
Close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1
(j) Make sure RTP-1, RTP-2, and RTP-3 (if installed) displays have changed (toggled).
(k) Release the RTP-1 Frequency Transfer switch.
(l) Release the Radio Tuning switch (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2).

2. Iridium SATCOM System - Operational Test
A. General
(1) This task gives the instructions to do an operational test of the Iridium Satellite
Communications System (SATCOM) system.
(2) This test includes these tests:
(a) SATCOM BITE Test
(b) Satellite Link Test
(c) SATCOM Voice Communication Test
(d) SATCOM Link Test
(3) The airplane must be outside of the hangar and away from any structures that may block the
view of the Iridium satellites from the antenna.

G. SATCOM Voice Communication Test
SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-003
(1) Do the steps that follow to do a SATCOM voice communication test for Air-to-Ground call from
the captain's station:
NOTE: This test verifies the interface between the SDU and the audio system, and also
verifies the SIM card activation status.
(a) Do a telephone number entry at the CDU-L:
1) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page.
2) Select the LSK adjacent to DIRECTORY> to show the SAT DIRECTORY INDEX
page.
3) Select the LSK adjacent to COMPANY> to show the AOC DIR DETAILS for
COMPANY page.
4) Select the LSK-L adjacent to the telephone number that is authorized to do this test.
5) Make sure that the selected telephone number's name/ID is entered below the
SAT-1 “<MAKE CALL”
(b) Do these steps at the captain's Audio Control Panel (ACP):
1) Push the applicable audio volume controls to make all the receive lights go off.
2) Push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch.
3) Turn on the SAT-1 volume control and set it to approximately the middle position.
(c) Do these steps at the First Officer (F/O)s ACP:
1) Turn off the SAT-1 volume control.
(d) Set the volume knob on the captain's cockpit speaker to the middle level or normal
comfortable listening setting.
23-15-40
(e) On CDU-L, select the LSK adjacent to <MAKE CALL for SAT-1.
NOTE: The status of SAT-1 changes from READY - DIALING - RINGING - ANSWERED.
(f) When the call is connected, make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 comes on at each
ACP .
(g) Make voice communication from captain's station with a handheld microphone and
cockpit speakers.
NOTE: You must push the Push-to-T alk (PTT) when you talk or transmit your voice.
(h) Make sure that the audio quality from both sides (cockpit and other end) is good.
NOTE: A delay may exist and it is not considered a problem. Any unacceptable level of
noise may be caused by the audio wire installation. In this condition, end the call
by selecting <END CALL on the CDU, and repeat this procedure with a different
telephone number, and verify the audio quality. If the audio quality improves with
the new telephone number, then this is not a problem.
(i) T ell the ground operator that the call will be completed.
(j) To end the call, select the LSK adjacent to <END CALL for SAT-1 on the CDU-L.
1) Make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 goes off at each ACP .
(k) On captain's ACP , push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch to make the MIC light go
off.
SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-004
(2) Do the steps that follow to do a SATCOM voice communication test for Ground-to-Air call from
the F/Os station:
(a) Do these steps at the F/Os ACP:
1) Push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch.
2) Turn on the SAT-1 volume control and set it to approximately the middle position.
(b) Do these steps at the Captain's ACP:
1) Turn off the SAT-1 volume control.
2) Push the VHF-1 microphone selector switch.
3) Turn on the VHF-1 volume control and set it to approximately the middle position.
(c) Set the volume knob on the F/Os cockpit speaker to the middle level or normal
comfortable listening setting.
(d) Use VHF-1 radio to tell the ground operator to initiate a SATCOM call.
(e) Make sure that a single tone chime is heard when the Ground-to-Air call is received.
(f) Make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 comes on at each ACP .
(g) On CDU-L, make sure that the status of SAT-1 shows ANSWERED.
(h) Make voice communication from F/Os station with a handheld microphone and cockpit
speakers.
NOTE: You must push the PTT when you talk or transmit your voice.
(i) Make sure that the audio quality from both sides (cockpit and other end) is good.
NOTE: A delay may exist and it is not considered a problem. Any unacceptable level of
noise may be caused by the audio wire installation. In this condition, end the call
by selecting <END CALL on the CDU, and repeat this procedure with a different
telephone number, and verify the audio quality. If the audio quality improves with
the new telephone number, then this is not a problem.
23-15-40
(j) T ell the ground operator that the call will be completed.
(k) To end the call, select the LSK adjacent to <END CALL for SAT-1 on the CDU-L.
1) Make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 goes off at each ACP .
(l) On F/Os ACP , push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch to make the MIC light go off.
H. SATCOM Link Test
SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-005
(1) Use the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) to do a
SATCOM Link Test:
NOTE: This test verifies the interface between the SDU and ACARS Communications
Management Unit (CMU).
(a) Push the MENU key on one of the CDUs to show the CDU “MENU” page.
1) Make sure that the <DLK prompt shows.
NOTE: It may take approximately up to 1 minute to display the <DLK” prompt after
the CMU is powered on.
(b) Select the LSK adjacent to <DLK to show the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page.
NOTE: If the CDU displays a page other than the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page, select
the LSK adjacent to the <RETURN" prompt until the DATALINK (DLK) MENU
page is displayed.
(c) Select the LSK adjacent to the MAINT> to show the CMU MAINTENANCE MENU page.
(d) Select the LSK adjacent to the <TEST to show the CMU TEST page.
(e) Select the LSKadjacent to the <INITIATE prompt under the SAT LINK prompt.
(f) Make sure that the word INITIATE, then the word TEST , then the word PASS, then the
word INITIATE shows on the display.
(g) Select the LSK adjacent to the <RETURN prompt until the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page
is displayed.
(h) Push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to show the CDU “MENU” page.

E. User Configuration Verifications and Changes
SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-007
(1) Read the procedure carefully before you make changes in the SAT USER ORT and SAT
CONFIG DB PN pages.
NOTE: You can cause a malfunction of the SATCOM system if you install the incorrect
configuration.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-008
(2) Use the center Control Display Unit (CDU) to get access to the SAT USER ORT page.
(a) Push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to show the CDU “MENU” page.
(b) Select the LSK adjacent to <SAT to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page.
(c) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page.
(d) Select the LSK adjacent to CONFIG> to show the SAT CONFIG 1/2 page.
(e) Enter the USER ORT password “ICG220A2” into the CDU scratch-pad.
NOTE: This is a password protected page. It is the operator's responsibility to record and
to keep the password if it is revised. The SATCOM supplier may charge a fee for
recovery of a lost password.
23-15-42
(f) Select the LSK adjacent to USER ORT> to show SAT USER ORT page.
1) Make sure that the CDU scratch-pad does not show one of these messages:
a) NOT ALLOWED
<1> If "NOT ALLOWED" message shows in the CDU scratch-pad, check the
connection between the CDU and SYS 1 AIR/GND relay.
b) INVALID PASSWORD
<1> If "INVALID PASSWORD" message shows in the CDU scratch-pad,
make sure that the password is correct.
<2> If the problem continues, contact the SATCOM supplier.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-210-003
(3) Examine the SAT USER ORT page.
(a) Make sure that the settings are the same as the settings that show in the SAT USER
ORT page (Figure 202).
NOTE: If the SAT USER ORT setting(s) in the airplane are different from the graphic,
revert to the default settings.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-003
(4) Clear faults.
(a) Press the CLEAR FAULTS> LSK to remove any fault indicators (Figure 203).
SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-004
(5) Change the USER ORT Password via the CDU.
(a) If it is necessary to change the default USER ORT password, do these steps:
NOTE: This page allows the user to modify the default USER ORT password to a
customized password. A customized password must be pre-approved by the
airlines engineering with a valid airplane engineering order.
1) Type the current password into the CDU scratch-pad.
2) Select USER ORT>LSK.
3) Enter the new password (up to 8 digits with 0-9 and A-Z) into the CDU scratch-pad.
4) Select USER ORT>LSK.
NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the
scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is
done.
5) Press SAVE>LSK to save changes.
6) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed
configuration.
NOTE: It is operator's responsibility to record and to keep the password if it is
changed. The SATCOM supplier may charge a fee for recovery of a lost
password.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-005
(6) Change the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) service
provider via the CDU.
(a) Enter the SAT USER ORT password in the CDU scratch-pad.
(b) Select USER ORT>LSK to access the SAT UORT CONFIG page.
(c) Press the <ACARS SRV LSK to toggle through the five available options (Figure 203):
23-15-42
1) GLOBALINK
2) ARINC DIRECT
3) HONEYWELL GDC
4) SITA
5) OTHER.
(d) Press SAVE> LSK to save changes.
NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the
scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is done.
(e) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed configuration.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-006
(7) Change the CALL DISPLAY via the CDU.
NOTE: The CALL DISPLAY type shows either the name or number of the caller. When the
<CALL DISPLAY is set BY NAME, the CDU will display the call recipient name if
located in the directory for ATG calls. The caller user ID is displayed for GTA calls.
NOTE: When the <CALL DISPLAY is set BY NUMBER, the CDU displays the number, which
may be a phone number or short code. Speed dial numbers are not displayed on the
CDU.
(a) The default settings is the dataloaded value. To change the display settings, do these
steps:
1) Enter the SAT USER ORT password in the CDU scratch-pad.
2) Select USER ORT>LSK to access the SAT UORT CONFIG page.
3) On the SAT UORT CONFIG page, press the <CALL DISPLAY LSK to toggle
between the BY NUMBER and BY NAME options.
4) Press SAVE> LSK to save changes.
NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the
scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is
done.
5) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed
configuration.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-007
(8) Change Entry Control via the CDU.
NOTE: The scratch-pad number entry control type has two possible configurations. ALL
NUMBERS and SHORT CODES. When <ENTRY CONTROL is set to ALL NUMBERS,
the CDU scratch-pad will accept long numbers, short codes, speed dial numbers and
station extensions.
NOTE: Selecting SHORT CODES does not permit any long numbers to be dialed. Speed dial
numbers will be accepted in the scratch-pad. Station-to-station calling is enabled with
both parameter settings.
(a) The default settings is the dataloaded value. To change the display settings, do these
steps:
1) Enter the SAT USER ORT password in the CDU scratch-pad.
2) Select USER ORT>LSK to access the SAT UORT CONFIG page.
3) On the SAT UORT CONFIG page, press the <ENTRY CONTROL LSK to toggle
between the ALL NUMBERS and SHORT CODE options.
23-15-42
4) Press SAVE> LSK to save changes.
NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the
scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is
done.
5) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed
configuration.
SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-008
(9) If the SAT UORT CONFIG page is correct and no change needed, do these steps:
(a) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU and return to the SAT UORT
CONFIG page.
(b) This procedure is completed.

SUBTASK 23-15-42-580-002
(1) Release the parking brakes.

SUBTASK 23-15-43-100-002

23-15-43
(a) Remove the protective caps from the coaxial cables connectors [3].

(c) Connect the coaxial cable connectors [3] to the antenna [1].
SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-003
(3) Put the antenna [1] in its installed position.
(a) Make sure that the forward orientation label points to the front of the airplane.
(b) Make sure that the four bolt holes on the antenna [1] align with the four bolt holes on the

NOTE: Fasteners can be used to help align the gasket onto antenna and antenna onto
fuselage.
SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-004
(4) Do these steps to install three of the four mounting bolts [2] to attach the antenna [1] on the
airplane:
NOTE: Leave the last bolt (#4 bolt) out for the electrical bond check.
(a) Apply a thin layer of Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, on the threads and shank to the
three mounting bolts [2].
(b) Loosely install the three mounting bolts [2] to attach the antenna [1] on the airplane.
(c) Tighten the three bolts [2] to 30.0 in-lb (3.4 N·m) with the torque wrench, STD-1014.
SUBTASK 23-15-43-760-001
(5) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the antenna base and airplane structure
(SWPM 20-20-00).
NOTE: Use the empty mounting bolt hole to get access to the antenna base.
(a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
(b) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less.
SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-005
(6) Do these steps to install the last mounting bolt [2] to attach the antenna [1] on the airplane:
(a) Apply a thin layer of Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, on the threads and shank to the
mounting bolt [2].
(b) Tighten the bolt [2] to 30.0 in-lb (3.4 N·m) with the torque wrench, STD-1014.

the surface smooth and remove unwanted material.
(e) If it is necessary, use wiper, G50262, wet with isopropyl alcohol to make the surface
smooth and remove unwanted material.

G. Underwater Locating Device (LF-ULD) Functional Test Using ULD Tester
SUBTASK 23-25-03-720-001
(1) Turn on the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616.
(2) Connect the activation cord to the Universal Serial Bus (USB) receptacle of the ultrasonic test
set, COM-15616.
(3) Select the measurement button (triangle with a circle) on the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616
screen.
(a) The connecting screen will appear on the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen.
NOTE: “ Reading incomplete retry in 66 sec.” error is displayed on the ULD tester screen

“Check polarity retry in 66 sec.” error is displayed on the ULD tester screen if
activation cord plug is not touching the proper polarity of the ULD.
SUBTASK 23-25-03-720-002
(4) Touch the activation cord plug to the correct poles of the ULD and wait 3 minutes.
(a) Make sure that the acoustic signal confirms successful completion and the data is
displayed on ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen.
(b) Make sure that on the right top of ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen that the Pulse
symbol blinks simultaneously to transmitted pulse.
(c) Make sure the battery voltage is not below 3.2 VDC.
1) If the voltage falls below 3.2 VDC, do this task: LOW FREQUENCY UNDERWATER
LOCATING DEVICE (LF-ULD) - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION,
PAGEBLOCK 23-25-03/401.
SUBTASK 23-25-03-100-001

F . Prepare for the Functional Test
SUBTASK 23-25-03-010-003
WARNING
USE CAUTION WHEN YOU OPEN THE NOSE RADOME COMPARTMENT . IF

G. Low Frequency Underwater Locating Device (LF-ULD) Functional and Battery Test Using
Multimeter
SUBTASK 23-25-03-710-001
(1) Do an operational check of the ULD.
(a) Do these steps to test the ULD in operation mode:
1) Connect the electrical jumper, STD-4931, wire ends to the ULD water switch pins
for approximately three seconds.
NOTE: The ULD contains an intelligent activation feature which avoids
unintentional activation, whereby the durability of the battery could be
impaired.
2) After three seconds, listen for the acoustic signal.
3) Remove the electrical jumper, STD-4931, and the acoustic signal will continue to
pulse every ten seconds for 60 seconds.
NOTE: The ULD is in the service operation mode for 60 seconds. The battery
voltage is circulating to the water switch pins and the ULD is pulsating the
ultrasonic signal. After 60 seconds, the ULD drops back into the sleep
mode.
SUBTASK 23-25-03-710-002
(2) Do these steps to check the ULD battery:
(a) Adjust a digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, to the 20 VDC range and resistance of 10
MOhm.
(b) Within 60 seconds, connect the probes of the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, to the
ULD water switch pins.
(c) Make sure that the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, displays a minimum of 3.2 VDC.
NOTE: It is normal for the voltage fluctuate in the decimal range. The pulsing of the
ultrasonic signal from the ULD causes a minor drop in voltage. The minimum
value must not fall below 3.2 VDC.
1) If the voltage falls below 3.2 VDC, replace the ULD.
(d) Remove the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793.
(e) Make sure that the water switch pins on the ULD has no grease or dirt.
1) If necessary, clean the switch pins with water, G50256, and general detergent,
B50117.
H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 23-25-03-410-003
(1) Do this task: Nose Radome - Close, TASK 53-52-00-410-802.

3. ACARS Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader (PDL)
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to install software in the Aircraft Communications Addressing And
Reporting System (ACARS) with a Portable Data Loader (PDL).
(2) The ACARS management unit must contain these pieces of software:
(a) Core and Application software.
(b) Airplane Database software.
(3) The Core and Application software must be installed before the Airplane Database software.
(4) A PDL and a Control Display Unit (CDU) are necessary for this procedure. A data loader
control panel and a PDL interface connector are also necessary. The data loader control panel
is installed above the DATA TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the P61 panel.
(5) A PDL is not a Boeing supplied part. Refer to the PDL supplier for instructions for operation.
The PDL will have a disk drive for software installation from disks. Some PDL have an internal
mass storage device. If the software is stored in the PDL, then disks are not necessary.
(6) Make sure the airplane is on the ground with the engines shutdown before you install the
software.
(7) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer
are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as
preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders
procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current
capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.
B. References
Reference Title
20-15-11-400-801 On-Airplane Software - Installation (P/B 201)

that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

3. ACARS Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader (PDL)
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to install software in the Aircraft Communications Addressing And
Reporting System (ACARS) with a Portable Data Loader (PDL).
(a) The ACARS Communications Management Unit (CMU) must contain these pieces of
software:
1) Core software
2) Operational software
3) Customer Unique Database software
(b) The ACARS Operational software must be installed before the Customer Unique
Database software. If the Operational software is already installed, then you can install
only the Customer Unique Database software.
(2) A PDL and a Control Display Unit (CDU) are necessary for this procedure. A data loader
control panel and a PDL interface connector are also necessary. The data loader control panel
is installed above the DATA TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the P61 panel.
(3) A PDL is not a Boeing supplied part. Refer to the PDL supplier for instructions for operation. A
PDL has a disk drive for software installation from disks. Some PDL have an internal mass
storage device. If the software is stored in the PDL, then disks are not necessary.
(4) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can install software.
(5) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer
are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as
preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders
procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current
capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.

NORM or NORMAL.

SUBTASK 23-27-33-860-031

NOTE: After the software/database is loaded, additional steps are necessary. This is to make

after a data load.

all the LED lights have come on.
NOTE: The LED lights are also on the CMU front panel.

NOTE: The CMU is ready for the operation.

2. ACARS Airplane Personality Module (APM) Data Verification Check
A. General
(1) This data verification check makes sure the Airplane Personality Module (APM) contains the
correct data values.
(2) A Flight Management Control Display Unit (CDU) is necessary for this procedure.
B. References
Reference Title
23-27-35-020-801 ACARS Airplane Personality Module (APM) Removal (P/B 401)

(P/B 501)

SUBTASK 23-27-35-840-001
(1) Make sure that the Flight Management Computer System (FMCS) is serviceable
(TASK 34-61-00-710-801).
SUBTASK 23-27-35-730-005
(2) Do these steps on CDU-2 to do an APM software verification check:
(a) Push the MENU key on both CDUs.
1) Make sure that the MAIN menu shows on both CDUs.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) adjacent to the <DLK prompt on CDU-1 and CDU-2.
1) Make sure that the DATALINK (DLK) MENU shows on the two CDU displays.
(c) Push the LSK adjacent to the MAINT> prompt.
(d) Push the LSK adjacent to the <APM prompt.
NOTE: Initial versions of the Communications Management Unit (CMU) software will not
show the airline identification (ID) on the CDU APM menu pages.
1) Make sure that the International Civil Aviation Organization (ICAO) address (Mode
S address) or code, airline ID and airplane registration number are correct.
2) If the ICAO address (Mode S address) or code, airline ID or airplane registration
number are not correct, do this task: ACARS Airplane Personality Module (APM)
Software Installation, TASK 23-27-35-700-802 or replace the Aircraft
Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) APM
(TASK 23-27-35-020-801).
23-27-35
(e) Push the LSK 6L twice to get back to DATALINK (DLK) MENU.

(a) Push the MENU key on both CDUs.

1) Make sure that the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page shows on the CDU display.

1) Make sure that the CMU MAINTENANCE page shows on the CDU display.

1) Make sure that CMU DEBUG page is displayed.
(f) Push the LSK adjacent to VDRS.
1) Make sure that ACARS VDRS DEBUG menu is displayed.

use.
• BC ICAO code: __________________.

23-27-35

1) Make sure that the CMU INIT page is displayed.

1) Make sure that CMU AIRLINE ID INIT page is displayed.

NOTE: Manual entry of Airline ID is required for the CMU.
(n) Select LSK 2L MANUAL.

1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page shows on the CDU display.

(v) Push the LSK adjacent to the CONTINUE> prompt.
1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page 1 of 4 (1/4) shows on the CDU display.

1) Make sure that the CMU AIRCRAFT TYPE shows on the CDU display.
2) Push the LSK adjacent to the <B737NG.
3) Push the LSK adjacent to the <RETURN.
(x) Use the CDU-2 keypad to enter the registration number of the airplane as shown on the
captain’s or first officer’s main instrument panel placard in the scratchpad.
NOTE: Enter 7 characters. If the registration number is less than 7 characters, enter
leading decimal points. Example: ..N1239 or .A-2345.
NOTE: If the registration number is more than 7 characters, it is necessary to enter the
registration number without the dash (-). Example: UPB3743.

1) If B738 is not displayed then enter B738 in the scratchpad and select LSK 5L.

1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page 3 of 4 (3/4) shows on the CDU display.

2. Passenger Address System - Operational Test
A. General
(1) The operational test is a fast check of the system. The test has a number of different parts. All
parts of the test follow in a given sequence.
(2) The operational test has these test sections:
(a) Boarding Music Operation Test
(b) Prerecorded Announcement Test
RYR 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236,
239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999
(c) Emergency Announcement Test
RYR ALL
(d) Passenger Address Operation Test
(e) Chime Operation Test

23-31-00-730-801Passenger Address System - System TestPDF p. 211

3. Passenger Address System - System Test
A. General
(1) The system test has these test sections:
(a) A ground mode test which checks the sound level of each passenger address speaker.
(b) A flight mode test which makes sure that the sound level of the passenger address
speakers increases when the engines are running.
(2) This test makes sure the sound level of the speakers in the passenger cabin increases by at
least 5 dBs when the engines are running.
23-31-00
(3) This test checks the sound level in the airplane with the engine running relay from the #1
engine, or the #1 and #2 engines, connected to the PA amplifier.
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

measure long cable distances)

(h) Measure the sound level across the speaker.

SUBTASK 23-31-03-710-001

(b) Push the call button on a PSU one more time.
1) Make sure that the PSU Call Light goes OFF .

3. Tape Head Cleaning
A. General
(1) This task cleans the tape path and the heads on the tape reproducer.
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00130 Alcohol - Isopropyl TT-I-735
G00034 Cotton Wiper - Process Cleaning Absorbent
Wiper (Cheesecloth, Gauze)
AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1
(Supersede BMS15-5
CL A)
G01659 Swab - Cotton Or Rayon, (Disposable)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
221 Passenger Compartment - Aft of Control Compartment to Forward Entry Door - Left
D. Cleaning Procedure
SUBTASK 23-31-07-020-003
(1) Do these steps to remove the cassette:
(a) Push the buttons 9 and 0 on the tape reproducer.
(b) Push the MUSIC 1 button.
1) Make sure the cassette door opens.
(c) Remove the tape cassette.
SUBTASK 23-31-07-160-001
(2) Do these steps to clean the tape reproducer heads and the tape path:
(a) Make the swab, G01659, or cotton wiper, G00034, moist with alcohol, B00130.
(b) Carefully clean the tape head or tape path with the swab, G01659, or cotton wiper,
G00034, until there is no change in the color of the applicator.
(c) Let the cleaned surfaces dry fully.
23-31-07
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103 (Continued)
SUBTASK 23-31-07-420-005
(3) Do these steps to install the cassette:
(a) Put the cassette into the tape reproducer.
1) Make sure the cassette is against the tape reproducer head.
NOTE: The tape is installed correctly when you see the label on the cassette.
(b) Close the door on the tape reproducer.
E. Boarding Music Test
SUBTASK 23-31-07-710-013
(1) Do this test of the boarding music:
(a) Push the MUSIC 1 button on the tape reproducer.
1) Make sure the MUSIC 1 light comes on.
(b) Push the START button.
1) Make sure the START light comes on.
(c) Adjust the volume control if it is necessary.
1) Make sure you hear clear music on all of the speakers.

Open original AMM PDF - page 261 ↗ 
4. Pre-Recorded Announcement Machine (PRAM) Maintenance Modes
A. General
(1) You use the PRAM maintenance modes to make adjustments to the announcement playback
operation. These are the maintenance modes:
RYR 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236,
239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; AIRPLANES WITH PART NUMBERS
RD-AX7360-XX
(a) UPDATE FILES - used to load, delete, or alter announcement or music files in memory.
RYR 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236,
239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; AIRPLANES WITH PART NUMBERS
RD-AX7360-XX OR RD-AX7370-01
(b) EMERGENCY REPEAT - sets the number of times that the emergency decompression
announcement repeats when triggered.
(c) PLAY MODE SETTING - selects single playback mode or sequential playback mode.
(d) MUSIC LEVEL SETTING - sets the boarding music volume level.
(e) ANNC LEVEL SETTING - sets the announcement volume level.

D. Prepare for the Operational Test
SUBTASK 23-42-00-840-001
(1) If it is necessary, do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811.
SUBTASK 23-42-00-840-002
(2) Make sure that all handsets are on the hook.
E. Cabin Interphone Operational Test
SUBTASK 23-42-00-710-001
(1) Do the steps that follow for the Cabin Interphone Call Test:
(a) Push the “ATTEND” call switch on the P5 panel (passenger sign panel).
(b) Make sure that the attendant call light on the forward and aft exit locator signs turn on.
(c) Make sure that you hear a HI/LO tone in the cabin.
(d) Remove any handset from the holder to reset the light.
(e) Make sure that the attendant call lights turn off.
(f) Put the handset back on its holder.
(g) Do the steps that follow for the forward attendant handset:
1) Remove the handset from its holder.
2) Push the button corresponding to “PILOT” on the handset.
3) Make sure that the Captain CALL light on the P5 panel turns on.
4) Make sure that you hear a HI tone in the flight deck.
23-42-00
5) Push the “RESET” button on the handset.
6) Push the button corresponding to an “Attendant call” on the handset.
7) Make sure that the attendant call light at the other station turns on.
8) Make sure you hear a HI/LO tone in the cabin.
9) Put the handset back on its holder.
10) Make sure that the attendant call light at the other station turns off.
(h) Repeat the test for the aft attendant handset(s).
SUBTASK 23-42-00-710-002
(2) Do the steps that follow for the Cabin Interphone Communication Test:
(a) Push the interphone button on all audio control panels.
NOTE: Button may be labeled “SVC” or “CABIN”.
(b) Make sure that you get good two way interphone communication between all
communication devices: flight deck handset, pilots boom/mic headset, forward and aft
attendant handsets.
F . Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 23-42-00-840-003
(1) If it is necessary, do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.

SUBTASK 23-42-00-710-005

installation:
(a) Do the steps that follow for any forward attendant handset:
1) Remove the handset from its holder.
2) Push the button corresponding to an “Attendant call” on the handset.
3) Make sure the attendant call light at the other station turns on.
4) Make sure you hear a HI/LO tone in the cabin.
5) Make sure you get a good two way communication between the handset and the
other station's attendant handset.
6) Push the “RESET” button on the handset.
23-42-00

8) Make sure the Captain CALL light on the P5 panel turns on.
9) Make sure you hear a HI tone in the flight deck.
10) Make sure you get a good two way communication between the handset and the
pilot's boom microphone.
11) Push the “RESET” button on the handset.
12) Put the handset back on its holder.
(b) Repeat the test for any aft attendant handset.

23-42-03-470-801Attendant Control Panel (ACP) Software LoadingPDF p. 303
Open original AMM PDF - page 303 ↗ 
2. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) Software Loading
A. General
(1) This procedure installs new Loadable Software Airplane Part (LSAP)s to the Attendant Control
Panel (ACP)'s and the Light Line Replaceable Unit (LRU)'s.
NOTE: You need to have three different types of software for the ACP operation: Operational
Software (OPS), Configuration Database (CDB) and Lighting Database (LDB). The
software is loaded individually.
NOTE: If you need to load software to only the forward ACP Mass Storage Area (MSA),
Airborne Data Loader (ADL) to ACP ( Phase 1) dataload is required. If you need to
load software to the aft ACP or the light LRU's, ACP to ACP/ACP to LRU (Phase 2)
dataload is required.
• A Phase 1 Data Load will transmit data from the aircraft loader to the FWD ACP . It
will also install software from the Network File Server (NFS)/Portable Data Loader
(PDL) to the FWD ACP MSA.
• A Phase 2 Data Load will install software from the MSA to the FWD ACP . The
software will automatically install from the FWD ACP to the AFT ACP and supply its
data to the Lighting LRUs. The AFT ACP and/or Lighting LRUs could possibly not be
available. Thus, you must manually install the software from the FWD ACP to the
AFT ACP . Also, you must manually supply the software data to Lighting LRUs.
B. References
Reference Title
20-15-11-400-801 On-Airplane Software - Installation (P/B 201)
32-09-00 P/B 201 AIR/GROUND SYSTEM - MAINTENANCE PRACTICES
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-940-001
(1) Make sure that the airplane is set to ground mode (PAGEBLOCK 32-09-00/201).
23-42-03
SUBTASK 23-42-03-940-002
(2) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2
Row Col Number Name
A 9 C00923 DATA LOADER
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
D 12 C01936 ATTENDANT PANELS
SUBTASK 23-42-03-470-001
(3) AIRPLANES WITH P1, P2 OR P4 SOFTWARE:
Do the steps that follow to install the new software:
(a) Set up the data loader at P61-1 panel located in the flight deck to applicable configuration
for loading software to the ACP (TASK 20-15-11-400-801).
1) Set CHANNEL SELECT to CENTER
2) Set DATA LOAD SELECT to ACP .
(b) Go to the forward ACP touchscreen and select Maintenance Menu.
(c) Enter password if required.
NOTE: Default password is “1234” unless changed by the operator.
(d) Select the “ADL to ACP” button located under the Data Load section.
1) The “ADL to ACP” button should be highlighted and the ACP is ready to accept data
loads from the data loader.
(e) Insert the media containing the new LSAPs into the data loader.
NOTE: The word “media” defines a data storage device. Examples of media are
Compact Disc (CD)s, mass storage devices, diskettes, Personal Computer
Memory Card Interface Adapter (PCMCIA), etc.
(f) Wait and check to make sure that the loading is complete for all the new LSAPs.
NOTE: The ACP will not accept a LSAP currently installed unless the file is corrupted. A
data load fail message will appear if the user tries to reload a LSAP which is
already installed.
(g) Press “ADL to ACP” button to exit the Data load.
1) The “ADL to ACP” button should not be illuminated and the “ACP to LRUs” button
should be disabled.
(h) Remove and store away the media containing the LSAPs.
(i) Press “ACP to ACP” button to load the LSAPs from the forward ACP to the aft ACP .
1) The “ACP to ACP” button should be highlighted.
NOTE: If the aft ACP has the same LSAPs as the forward ACP , the ACP to ACP
button will fail and a manual restart of the ACP circuit breaker is required.
(j) Wait for the loading to complete, both the forward and aft ACP's should restart.
(k) For loading data from the ACP to the Light Emitting Diode (LED) Lighting assemblies, do
the steps that follow:
1) Press the “ACP to LRUs” button.
23-42-03
a) The “ACP to LRUs” button should highlight.
2) Wait for the data load to finish.
3) Check to make sure that the Address, Scene and Zone information are downloaded
without errors.
SUBTASK 23-42-03-470-002
(4) AIRPLANES WITH P5 SOFTWARE:
Do the steps that follow to install the new software:
(a) Set up the data loader at P61-1 panel located in the flight deck to applicable configuration
for loading software to the ACP (TASK 20-15-11-400-801).
1) Set CHANNEL SELECT to CENTER
2) Set DATA LOAD SELECT to ACP .
(b) Go to the forward ACP touchscreen and select Maintenance Menu.
(c) Enter password if required.
NOTE: Default password is “1234” unless changed by the operator.
(d) Select the “Data Load” button in the Maintenance menu.
NOTE: When you push the “Data Load” button, you will see four buttons in the control
window: Phase 1 Data Load, Phase 2 Data Load, Start, and Exit. Data loading
instructions will be displayed in the result window.
• A Phase 1 Data Load will transmit data from the aircraft loader to the FWD
ACP . It will also install software from the NFS/PDL to the FWD ACP MSA.
• A Phase 2 Data Load will install software from the MSA to the FWD ACP . The
software will automatically install from the FWD ACP to the AFT ACP and
supply its data to the Lighting LRUs. The AFT ACP and/or Lighting LRUs could
possibly not be available. Thus, you must manually install the software from the
FWD ACP to the AFT ACP . Also, you must manually supply the software data
to Lighting LRUs.
(e) For Phase 1 Data Load, do the steps that follow:
1) Push the “Phase 1 Data Load” button.
2) Insert the media containing the new LSAPs into the data loader or select an
appropriate file in the Electronic Aircraft Data Loader (EADL).
NOTE: Once the process has started, the result window will show the current
progress of the file transfer. The status bar will show the approximate
percent complete as the process executes.
3) When the process completes, push the “Exit” button.
(f) For Phase 2 Data Load, do the steps that follow:
1) Push the “Phase 2 Data Load” button.
NOTE: When user select Phase 2 Data Load button, the screen will show the
LSAP selection table and control window. The current configuration is
selected at default. The user can select another LSAP to load into the
ACP's and the light LRU's.
2) Select the LSAP in the MSA.
23-42-03
3) Push the “Start” button.
NOTE: Once the process has started, the result window will show the current
progress of the file transfer. The status bar will show the approximate
percent complete as the process executes.
NOTE: "out of sequence" is a data load message and can be ignored only if there
are no other faults during the data load and there is no mismatch during the
configuration check.
SUBTASK 23-42-03-700-001
(5) Do this task to verify that the LSAPs are loaded correctly: Attendant Control Panel (ACP)
Configuration Check, TASK 23-42-03-700-801.

23-42-03-940-801Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Data Diskette PreparationPDF p. 308

5. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Data Diskette Preparation
A. General
(1) This procedure prepares the diskette for saving configuration check data and fault data.
NOTE: This task is applicable for airplanes with P5 software.
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-940-004
(1) To prepare a diskette for saving configuration check data, do the steps that follow on your
computer:
(a) Insert the diskette in the diskette drive.
(b) Create a text file with following contents:
LBL=226
DSN = 1
DTN = 1
23-42-03
B1D = 1H,1H,374
B2D = 0H,0H,300
B1P = 1
B2P = 0
B1R = SAVECFG.TXT
(c) Save the text file on the diskette as CONFIG.LDR.
NOTE: Windows may hide the extension of the text file. Make sure the filename has a
“.LDR” file extension and not a “.TXT” extension.
(d) Remove the diskette from diskette drive.
SUBTASK 23-42-03-940-003
(2) To prepare a diskette for saving fault data and configuration check data, do the steps that
follow on your computer:
(a) Insert a diskette in the diskette drive.
(b) Create a text file with following contents:
LBL=226
DSN = 1
DTN = 1
B1D = 1H,1H,374
B2D = 0H,0H,300
B1P = 1
B2P = 0
B1R = SAVEALL.TXT
(c) Save the text file on the diskette as CONFIG.LDR.
NOTE: Windows may hide the extension of the text file. Make sure the filename has a
“.LDR” file extension and not a “.TXT” extension.
(d) Remove the diskette from diskette drive.
23-42-03-970-801Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Configuration DataPDF p. 309
Open original AMM PDF - page 309 ↗ 
6. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Configuration Data
A. General
(1) This task saves the Configuration Data on a diskette.
NOTE: This task is applicable for airplanes with P5 software.
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-970-001
(1) Do the steps that follow to save the Configuration Data:
(a) In the Maintenance menu column, push the “Config Check” button.
NOTE: When you push the Config Check button, three buttons will appear in the control
window: Start Check, Save Config Data and Exit. The result window will display
instructions for user to view or save the configuration data.
(b) Insert a properly formatted diskette into the Aircraft Data Loader (ADL).
23-42-03
1) The Save Data diskette can be created by using the Configuration Database
Generator (CDG) or by the task: Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Data
Diskette Preparation, TASK 23-42-03-940-801.
(c) Push the Save Config Data button in the control window.
NOTE: When this process starts, the result window will display the current progress of
the file transfer.
(d) Push the Exit button in the control window to complete the process.
23-42-03-970-802Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Fault DataPDF p. 310
Open original AMM PDF - page 310 ↗ 
7. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Fault Data
A. General
(1) This task saves the system test faults or data load faults on a diskette.
NOTE: This task is applicable for airplanes with P5 software.
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-970-002
(1) In the Maintenance menu column, push the ‘‘Config Check’’ button.
NOTE: NOTE: When you push the Config Check button, three buttons will appear in the
control window: Start Check, Save Config Data and Exit. The result window will display
instructions for user to view or save the configuration data. This will save the current
config data to memory.
(2) To save the fault data, do the steps that follow:
(a) In the Maintenance menu column, push the Fault Data button.
NOTE: When you push the Fault Data button, four buttons will appear in the control
window: System Test Faults, Data Load Faults, Save All and Exit. The result
window will display instructions for user to view or save the fault data.
(b) Insert a properly formatted diskette into the Aircraft Data Loader (ADL).
1) The Save Data diskette can be created by using the Configuration Database
Generator (CDG) or by the task: Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Saving Data
Diskette Preparation, TASK 23-42-03-940-801.
(c) Push the Save All button in the control window.
NOTE: When this process starts, the result window will display the current progress of
the file transfer.
(d) Push the Exit button in the control window to complete the process.
23-42-03-070-801Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Erasing Mass Storage Area (MSA)PDF p. 310
Open original AMM PDF - page 310 ↗ 
8. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) - Erasing Mass Storage Area (MSA)
A. General
(1) This task erases the Mass Storage Area (MSA) at the forward ACP .
NOTE: This task is applicable for airplanes with P5 software.
23-42-03
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-070-001
(1) At the forward ACP , do the steps that follow to erase the MSA:
(a) In the ACP main menu column, push the Maintenance button.
(b) Enter password if required.
NOTE: Default password is “1234” unless changed by the operator.
(c) In the Maintenance menu column, push the Data Load button.
(d) In the control window, push the Phase 2 Data Load button.
(e) In the control window, push the Erase MS Area button.
(f) In the control window, push the Start button.
NOTE: The result window will show the progress of the erase process.

9. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) Touchscreen Cleaning
A. General
(1) This tasks tells you how to clean the LCD touchscreen.
B. LCD Touchscreen Cleaning Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-160-001
(1) Do the steps that follow to clean the LCD Touchscreen:
(a) Under display menu, push the Clean screen button to put the ACP in screen cleaning
mode.
(b) Clean the LCD Touchscreen assembly with soft, lint-free cloth and isopropyl alcohol.
1) Wipe the screen in gentle motion.
NOTE: Lint-free cleaning wipes with alcohol are acceptable.
23-42-03-700-802Attendant Control Panel (ACP) LCD Screen Display CheckPDF p. 311
Open original AMM PDF - page 311 ↗ 
10. Attendant Control Panel (ACP) LCD Screen Display Check
A. General
(1) This task determines serviceability of the ACP LCD screen displays.
B. References
Reference Title
25-25-11-000-802 Attendant's Panel with LCD Touch Panel - Removal (P/B 401)
25-25-11-400-802 Attendant's Panel with LCD Screen - Installation (P/B 401)
C. LCD Screen Display Check Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-720-001
(1) Make sure that the LCD screen display is clear to read.
(2) Count the number of defective pixels on the LCD screen of the ACP .
23-42-03
(a) Make sure that there are no more than four defective pixels in total with no more than two
defective pixels adjacent to each other.
NOTE: Defective is defined as a pixel that does not display the intended color with
respect to adjacent pixels.
1) If the LCD screen does not meet this criteria, replace the ACP . Do these tasks:
a) Attendant's Panel with LCD Touch Panel - Removal, TASK 25-25-11-000-802
b) Attendant's Panel with LCD Screen - Installation, TASK 25-25-11-400-802.

2. Attendant Control Panel - Operational Test
A. General
(1) This task makes sure that the Attendant Control Panel (ACP) operates correctly.
(2) The ACP controls and monitors several systems on the airplane. The ACP operational test
consists of the tests for all systems that the ACP is associated with.
B. References
Reference Title
23-42-00-710-803 Cabin Interphone system - Attendant Panel Installation Test
(P/B 501)
23-42-03-700-801 Attendant Control Panel (ACP) Configuration Check (P/B 201)
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 23-42-03-700-004
(1) Do the power-up test for the ACP:
NOTE: After power-up, the Over-T emperature LED will turn on for 2 seconds and then turn off.
(a) Open this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
D 12 C01936 ATTENDANT PANELS
(b) Close this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
D 12 C01936 ATTENDANT PANELS
(c) Check the LED at the upper left corner of the Integrated Switch Assembly (ISA).
NOTE: The LED should turn on in the first 2 seconds after power-up and then turn off.
1) If the LED light (amber) does not turn on in the first 2 seconds after power-up, refer
to FIM 23-42 TASK 841 for corrective actions.
2) If the LED light continues to stay on after power-up, refer to FIM 23-42 TASK 839 for
corrective actions.
(d) Check the ACP Touch Screen to make sure that it responds to touching inputs.
1) If the ACP Touch Screen does not respond to any touching inputs, refer to
FIM 23-42 TASK 840 for corrective actions.
SUBTASK 23-42-03-710-003
(2) Do the test for the Cabin Interphone system:
(a) Do this task: Cabin Interphone system - Attendant Panel Installation Test,

SUBTASK 23-43-00-710-001
(2) Do an operational test of the forward attendant to aft attendant call:
(a) Do these steps from each forward attendant handset to each aft attendant handset.
1) Push the button for attendant on the handset at the forward attendant's station.
a) Make sure you hear a chime on the PA speakers.
b) Make sure the aft pink attendant call light comes on.
2) Push the button for reset on the handset at the aft attendant's station.
a) Make sure the attendant call light goes off.
3) Speak into the forward attendant's handset.
a) Make sure you can hear speech on the aft attendant's handset.
4) Speak into the aft attendant's handset.
a) Make sure you can hear speech on the forward attendant's handset.
SUBTASK 23-43-00-710-004

1) Push the button for attendant on the handset at the aft attendant's station.
a) Make sure you hear a chime on the PA speakers.
b) Make sure the pink attendant call light at the forward attendant's station comes
on.
2) Push the button for reset on the handset at the forward attendant's station.
a) Make sure the attendant call light goes off.

(4) Do a cabin interphone reset test:

heard over the PSU speaker.
(d) Release the PTT button.

b) Make sure the CALL light on the P5 overhead panel comes on.
2) Speak into the attendant's handset.
a) Make sure you can hear speech on the pilot's headset.
3) Speak into the pilot's boom microphone.
a) Make sure you can hear speech on the attendant's handset.

23-43-00

compartment.

(b) Release the GRD CALL button.
(c) Push and hold the PILOT CALL button on the external power P19 panel at the nose
wheel well area.
1) Make sure you hear a chime in the flight compartment.
2) Make sure the CALL light on the P5 overhead panel comes on.
(d) Release the pilot call BUTTON.

SUBTASK 23-51-00-860-002
(1) Make sure the SERVICE INTERPHONE switch on the captain's and first officer's overhead
panel is in the OFF position.
SUBTASK 23-51-00-860-006
(2) Set each Audio Control Panel (ACP) to these conditions:
(a) Push all audio monitor switches to off.
(b) Push the FLT microphone selector switch to on.
1) Make sure its light comes on.
(c) Push the volume control for the FLT microphone selector switch.
(d) Turn the volume control for the FLT microphone selector switch clockwise to the middle
position.
SUBTASK 23-51-00-860-004
(3) Set the captain's and first officer's ACPs to these conditions:
(a) Push the SPKR volume control switch to ON.
1) Make sure its light comes on.
(b) Turn the SPKR volume control switch clockwise to the middle position or to the volume
level you are comfortable with.
23-51-00
SUBTASK 23-51-00-710-001

(PTT) switch on the hand microphone:
(a) Push and hold the PTT switch on the hand microphone.
(b) Speak into the hand microphone.
1) Make sure you can hear the voice clearly from the other headsets.
2) Make sure the voice level from the flight deck speakers decreases.
(c) Release the PTT switch on the hand microphone.

(5) Do the communication test between each of the flight crew stations with the PTT switch on the
captain's and first officer's control wheel:

position.
